# Changelog — what each version changed in the files molbuilder writes

**Role:** record
**Domain:** process

One line per version, newest first: what changed in which file molbuilder
writes, and the date. The rule for when the version moves is
[`conventions.md`](?doc=process/conventions.md) § 2, *Versioning*; every deck
and run script records the version that wrote it
([`execution/job-contracts.md`](?doc=execution/job-contracts.md) § 3.2).

| version | date | what changed |
|---|---|---|
| **2.69.54** | 2026-11-13 | **A job's GPUs read by the one door wherever a group's members, a sequence's groups or a benchmark's trials are compared** — a last-number move ([`conventions.md`](?doc=process/conventions.md) § 2: fixes) — *(the overlap review's X-EXEC, its sixth finding)*. Whether a run uses a GPU and how many it asks for are two facts stated in two places, and one door reads them together, refusing a pair that disagrees (`model.gpu_request`, [`execution/gpu.md`](?doc=execution/gpu.md) § 1.1). Three readers took them apart again: a group's one job (`group.envelope`) compared the raw ask and the switch as two facts, so its refusal said *its GPUs None against 'gpu:1', GPU use False against True*; a sequence's one request (`group.sequence_request`) parsed each count from the raw ask; and a benchmark's shelves compared and copied the raw ask beside the door's switch. **Now** each asks the door: a group's members are compared on the GPUs each asks for, none said *0*, in place of the two fields the door reads together (`model.GPU_FIELDS`) — a member whose two disagree, which prep's own check refuses first, refused as the group's, naming it, as a sequence's group is and a shelf's trial; a sequence's counts and its one ask are the door's; a shelf's trials share the door's answer, and its one request carries it; and a benchmark's recommendation reads the count of the door's own ask with the door's own parser, where it summed every device the scheduler's parser finds. **What a person sees**: `prep task` of a group whose members ask different GPUs refuses *`medium` cannot share `coarse`'s job: its GPUs 0 against 1*, where it named the raw ask and the switch apart; nothing else. **Tests**: a row of the prep table (`tests/data/prep_protocol.toml`): a group of two, one member's run card asking a GPU — refused, each count said, nothing written — failing on the old code, its words the raw ask's. **Left**: the plan's one-line resources (`plan.resources_text`) show the scheduler's spelling, *gpu:1*, as a display. Run through the project's runner, on the final code: the prep table (84 rows passed) and the fast batch (6,029 run, none failed). |
| **2.69.53** | 2026-11-13 | **A held group's refusal said once, its stop a line of its own** — a last-number move ([`conventions.md`](?doc=process/conventions.md) § 2: fixes) — *(the overlap review's X-EXEC, its ninth finding)*. A group a sequence of groups still going holds is the job's: prep refuses to prepare it again (R-P23) and launch to launch it (R-L45). The two refusals were written twice — `ready.held_why` and `submit._held_by` — saying where the group goes in two wordings, and prep's ended its prose with the stop, *… or stop the job first: molbuilder jobs stop 4182*, where every printed command is one command a line ([`execution/job-system.md`](?doc=execution/job-system.md) § 5.3): the line pasted ran the prose. The groups' listing that `prep task` and `launch task` show put its command inside a parenthesis the same way, *(goes in job 4182 -- after group 1; molbuilder jobs status)*. **Now** one composer says the refusal (`ready.held_why`, `launching` for the launch's): where the group goes — the job, the sequence's state, after which group — what preparing it would rewrite, or when to launch it, and the stop on a line of its own under the sentence; and the listing names a held group's state alone, *(goes in job 4182 -- after group 1)*, the line that follows the sequence, `molbuilder jobs status`, a line of its own after the groups. **What a person sees**: `prep task` of a held group refuses saying after which group the job launches it, the stop command on its own line — and Task setup's note beside its box the same words; `launch task` of one says *… after group 1.  Launch it alone …* where it said *; launch it alone …*, with no blank line after; the groups' listing ends, when a sequence holds a group, *a sequence still going holds group 2 -- follow it:* (several, *groups 2, 3*; several jobs, *sequences … follow them*) and the command under it. **Removed**: `submit._held_by`, its two callers asking the one composer. **Untested** by an API test — a held group is made only by a sequence still running, so the change's only tests are end-to-end, which before F10 names it untested ([`plans/plan.md`](?doc=plans/plan.md), *How a release before F10 is checked*): the sequence's end-to-end tests on a real SIESTA run, revised (`test_what_the_job_holds_is_not_taken_from_it`, `test_a_group_the_job_holds_goes_in_it`) to pin a refusal's stop as a line of its own, the job's own, and the listing's `jobs status`, pass, and fail on the old code — prep's stop at the end of its prose and the listing's in its parenthesis. **Left**, the same rule elsewhere: a background start's line, *pid … ; to stop it: molbuilder jobs stop N*. Run through the project's runner, on the final code: the sequence's tests (14 passed, its end-to-end on a real run among them) and the fast batch (6,028 run, none failed). |
| **2.69.52** | 2026-11-12 | **A launch that names no group sends none changed since its prep** — a last-number move ([`conventions.md`](?doc=process/conventions.md) § 2: fixes) — *(the user, 2026-11-12, asked on 2.69.51's question)*. A group prepared, never launched, then changed by the person — a value of its template, its overrides, its run card — is prepared again before it runs: `status` says so, `prep task` offers it and Task setup's tab leads with its prep (2.69.51). A `launch task` that named no group still sent it, the files prepared before the change, the run computing what the person had changed away. **Now** what a launch with no group named offers asks the ready door whether the group needs a prep (`GroupAnswer.launchable` asks `needs_prep`), so such a group is not sent: wherever the launch lists the groups, each changed stage's line says *… -- prepare it again*, as `status` does; named, `launch task --group N`, it goes as asked — a named launch compares nothing ([`execution/job-system.md`](?doc=execution/job-system.md), *Changed since prepared*, *What `launch task` sends*). **What a person sees**: `molbuilder jobset launch task` with no group passes such a group by — refused when nothing else can go (R-L2, its last line now *each one above says why*), the changed stage saying its changes and *prepare it again*; or asking which, when several others can go, or which stage launched before to launch again, the groups listed; or sending the one other that can go — where it sent the changed group. **Tests**: two rows of the launch table (`tests/data/launch_protocol.toml`): group 1 prepared, its template then changed, group 2 prepared after — a launch naming no group is refused, the changed stage saying *prepare it again*, nothing written — failing on the old code, the launch sending group 1; and named, `--group 1`, it goes as asked. The test function first written for it, in `tests/test_status_lists_the_ladder.py`, retired into the rows. Run through the project's runner, on the final code: the launch table (11 rows passed) and the fast batch (6,028 run, none failed). |
| **2.69.51** | 2026-11-12 | **A group's lines on Task setup asked of the doors that answer them** — a last-number move ([`conventions.md`](?doc=process/conventions.md) § 2: fixes) — *(the overlap review's X-EXEC, its third finding)*. A group's tab on Task setup shows what a person types for it (`/api/task-setup/commands`): its prep and its launch, or its launch alone once it needs no prep. The route worked out *needs no prep* itself — every member prepared in one job, its run there — and so left out the rule `prep task` offers by: a group never launched and changed since its prep is prepared again ([`execution/job-system.md`](?doc=execution/job-system.md), *Changed since prepared*, the user, 2026-10-25). Such a group's tab showed its launch alone, which sends the files prepared before the change, while the page's own *Each group, and what is next* said to prepare it again. And a group no verb takes — its launched members changed, or a sequence still going holds it — showed the refusal's sentence where its contract says the next step as `status` prints it: for a held group, *prepare it once that job has ended, or stop the job* where `status` says where it stands and names `molbuilder jobs status`. **Now** the ready door answers *needs a prep* once (`GroupAnswer.needs_prep`), which `prep task`'s offer and the route ask, the contracts' restatements of the offer swept to it, and *no verb takes it* once (`GroupAnswer.no_verb_takes`), which `preparable` and the route ask; a stuck group's lines are its step from the status door (`runstatus.GroupStep`: its words, then its lines) ([`web/web-api.md`](?doc=web/web-api.md)). *Prepared as described* keeps its one meaning, every member prepared in the one job its members make. **What a person sees**: on Task setup, the tab of a group changed since its prep, never launched, leads with its prep line again; a group held by a sequence still going says where it stands and `molbuilder jobs status`, as `status` does. **Tests**: revised, `test_the_lines_task_setup_shows_are_the_terminals_and_are_taken`: a value changed by the person after the prep brings the group's prep line back, and every line is taken as typed — failing on the old code, its first line the launch. **Untested** by a test of its own: a stuck group's lines — a launched group whose members changed, or one a running sequence holds, made only by real launches — read against the status door's step. **Left, for the user**: a group never launched and changed since its prep is still sent by `launch task` with no group named (`ready.launch_offer`, *prepared as described*: what launch sends is what prep wrote, `job-system.md`, *Launch compares nothing*), and a launched group changed since its prep shows its launch alone in its tab, while `status` says to prepare it again for its job request (its run card changed) or gives the three lines that start it again (what it computes changed) — each is what its contract states today. **Left to the page doors** (O-STATE), measured: the Prep card's line under its boxes reads the groups' states itself, and with every box unticked calls every group prepared while one never launched and changed since its prep, or with a member's run gone, is offered. Run through the project's runner, on the final code: the Task setup pages' end-to-end tests with the ladder's and the printed commands' (39 passed) and the fast batch (6,026 run, none failed). |
| **2.69.50** | 2026-11-12 | **What Task setup proposes to write, written by the server's writers** — a last-number move ([`conventions.md`](?doc=process/conventions.md) § 2: fixes) — *(the overlap review's X-EXEC, its first finding)*. A folder holding a hand-over showed and saved a description and a job request the page put together itself, key by key, from the hand-over's pieces — its schema strings, its layout, its run, its structure, the proposed ladder, what it varies, each stage's group — and a description with no job request was given one assembled the same way from the folder answer's `request_schema` and `proposed_groups`; `molbuilder describe` writes the same two records through their writers, and two tests kept their own copies of the page's assembly. So the editor's proposal put the kind's key last where the file Save wrote puts it after the run, and any key the writers gain was one the page would not propose. **Now** the folder answer carries `proposed` — what a Save would write that the folder does not hold yet: a hand-over's description, the hand-over completed with what Task setup adds and read by the description's one reader (`task.from_text`, which now names what it reads in its refusals of the content too, as the job request's reader does), and its job request, the stages in the groups the hand-over proposes, read by the job request's; a description's missing job request, numbered by the one rule a new calculation's are — each in the one JSON spelling (`persist.json_text`), byte for byte what Save writes; the page shows and saves that text ([`web/handover-procedure.md`](?doc=web/handover-procedure.md) § 5, [`web/task-setup.md`](?doc=web/task-setup.md) § 9a, [`web/web-api.md`](?doc=web/web-api.md)). A hand-over's label, its kind's entry, its structure pair and its bench answer are that description's too; and a new calculation's job request -- `molbuilder describe`'s, and the one proposed for a description with none -- is made by one door, `describe.new_request`. **What a person sees**: in a hand-over's editor, the kind's line after the run's, where the saved file has it; and a hand-over edited by hand into one the description's or the job request's reader refuses is refused as its folder opens, in their words, rather than at Save. **Removed**: the page's `proposedFromHandover`, and its own refusal of a proposal that throws; the hand-over file's `task_schema`, `task_shape` and `job_request_schema`, written only for the page — a hand-over opens whether or not it carries them; the folder answer's `request_schema` and `proposed_groups`; its working a hand-over's label out from the pieces; the tests' own assemblies (`_as_the_page_saves`, the issues card's), now `support.road.as_the_page_saves`, which reads the proposal; `_default_groups_of`, in `describe.new_request`'s place. **Tests**: new, `test_what_the_page_shows_is_what_save_writes` (two rows: a hand-over; a description whose job request was deleted from the sidebar): the proposal, saved as the page saves it, is byte for byte what lands — failing on the old code, and on a proposal spelled another way; the hand-over test's layout check retired — Save refuses a description without the layout; revised, the hand-over's end-to-end test (`test_a_hand_over_is_finished_on_the_page_and_saved_as_both_records`): the description the editor shows is byte for byte the one Save writes. And new, `test_a_hand_over_its_readers_refuse_is_refused_as_it_opens`: a stage renamed by hand in the hand-over, through the sidebar's file editor, is refused as its folder opens, in the readers' words -- failing on this release's first draft, which built the job request outside the hand-over reader's refusal and failed the whole folder answer. Run through the project's runner, on the final code: the end-to-end tests of the Build, Spectrum and Task setup pages (69 passed) and the fast batch (6,026 run, none failed). |
| **2.69.49** | 2026-11-12 | **A setting named, worded and offered by one record on every page** — a last-number move ([`conventions.md`](?doc=process/conventions.md) § 2: fixes) — *(the overlap review's X-TMPL, its seventh finding, which closes it)*. Four records of one setting were built by hand: the Build form's field (`_shared._item_to_field`, its choices and stages laid after it by the schema's walk), and Task setup's three — a run card's row (`/api/task-setup/sweepable`), a stage column (`/api/task-setup/columns`) and a value shared by every stage (the folder answer's `template.shared`) — each with its own fallbacks: a missing label worded two ways, a missing unit three (`""`, `null`, left out), the choices narrowed by two rules; and the shared values' record carried no words for a value, so on a transport folder the card said *Use the electrodes' own bulk Hamiltonian* **true** where the stage cells and a run card's choices say a switch *on* or *off* (seen on the running server). The page held a fifth, copying eleven keys of the run card's record by hand. **Now** one door, `_shared.item_record` ([`web/form-schema.md`](?doc=web/form-schema.md) § 1.1), says what every page names and offers a setting by — its label (its name where it has none, as both contracts say), help, unit, the one default and its words, its control, the choices this kind takes on the surface's engines (`offered_by_any`, which gives what `offered` gives on one engine — measured on the 21 settings with choices, on every kind and engine carrying one, 49 pairs), each value's words, a blank's words, the rungs that read it — every key on every record, empty where the item has none; the form field and Task setup's three are that record with their own facts, and the page keeps the run card's record whole. **Removed**: the stage column's `group`, which nothing read -- the columns a table starts with are the proposed ladder's (`task.fresh_varies`). **What a person sees**: the shared card says a switch's value *on* / *off* ([`web/task-setup.md`](?doc=web/task-setup.md) § 3) — today one switch, a transport calculation's bulk electrodes; nothing else — measured: every surface's records before and after, over every engine, kind and form part: the run cards' unchanged, the columns' one change a missing unit sent as `null` for `""`, the form fields' and shared values' only keys added — on a form field present and empty where they had been left out, and a switch's words; on a shared value the facts its record had not carried, none drawn but a switch's words. **Tests**: new, `test_every_surface_names_a_setting_by_one_record` (four rows, an engine and a kind each): the form, the stage columns and the shared values say the same facts of a setting they share — failing on the old code, and on the column's missing unit spelled `""` again; revised, `test_an_optional_item_offers_its_unset_state_in_its_own_words`: a setting with no blank offers no words for one, its key now present and empty rather than left out. **Untested** by a test of its own: the shared card's drawing, and a switch's words on the shared record — no test opens Task setup on a transport folder, the one kind sharing a switch, and the new test's kinds share none — checked by clicks on the running server; and the run-card route, whose records no other surface serves — measured unchanged. **Left** to the page words (O-WORDS), measured: the Transport tab's locked switches said *true* / *false*, a run card's inherited value said as it is held (*— true (every stage)*) and its members' differing values too (*stated apart — device: true*), the defaults panel's own rows, and the three-way select's *yes* / *no* beside the cells' *on* / *off* — each a page's own rule for saying a value; and to the documents' pass (F10), the rule that a `stage` setting's *vary per stage* box starts ticked, stated seven times in four contracts (`form-schema.md`, `template.md`, `stages.md`, `vibration.md`) and drawn by no page since the column picker replaced the boxes. Run through the project's runner: the end-to-end and page tests of every path changed — the Build, Spectrum and Task setup pages, the charge and spin card, the renderer's own, and four Transport tab tests on a real junction (107 passed), before the reviews' fixes, which changed no record a form or a run card serves (measured) and took the column's unread `group` away; then, on the final code, Task setup's page tests with the tests of the records (177 passed) and the fast batch (6,023 run, none failed). |
| **2.69.48** | 2026-11-12 | **A form's fields read through the renderer: one walk, one map of ids** — a last-number move ([`conventions.md`](?doc=process/conventions.md) § 2: fixes) — *(the overlap review's X-TMPL, its sixth finding)*. A form field's id is the server's — the container's prefix and the item's name ([`web/form-schema.md`](?doc=web/form-schema.md) § 1) — and every page read it from the schema; but the pages walked the schema's sections each their own way: the Build, Spectrum and Transport tabs for the map of names to ids that places a finding beside its field ([`science/validation.md`](?doc=science/validation.md) R2), the Transport tab again for a field's label and for the values its panels are locked at, and the charge and spin card for its own fields; and the Build tab, to wire its live check, listed every input by the renderer's own rule for a triple's three cells and worked each id back to its engine by the server's prefixes, written out again (`p-`, `py-`), as its pseudopotential folder's caption found its field by one (`p-psml-lib`), polling until the form was drawn. **Now** the renderer, which owns what a schema says, holds the one walk, `formSchema.fields`, and the one map of ids built on it, `formSchema.fieldIds` ([`web/form-schema.md`](?doc=web/form-schema.md) § 3), which every page asks and the renderer's own readers too — collecting, saving, restoring, the defaults panel and what greys a field out; drawing alone goes section by section; the Build tab's live check listens on each engine's form whole — its container hears `input` and `change` from any field there, the renderer's own writes included, which bubble — and the caption is put in place once the forms are drawn and restored, its field found by the map; so no page walks a schema, keeps a list of ids, a prefix, or the triple's rule. **What a person sees**: nothing — a typed field's live check may ask once more when the field is left, as the Spectrum tab's already did, with the same answer; and the pseudopotential folder's caption appears however long the forms take to load, where the polling gave up after six seconds. **Removed**: the Build tab's `getFormIds`; the caption's polling loop, its own id, which nothing read, and its guard against a second install, now that it has one; `renderIssues`, which worked the engine back from the form's id for its one caller, `_showFindings`, which holds it. **Tests**: none new — the end-to-end tests of a finding beside its field and of the live checks (the Build and Spectrum tabs, the charge and spin card) pass, 61, and fail with the map answering nothing (seven) or the Build forms' listeners gone (four). **Untested** by a test of its own: the Transport tab's map, labels and locked values, and the caption — read equivalent (the same fields, in the same order, the last of a name winning as before), the caption checked by clicks on the running server. Run through the project's runner, on the final code: the fast batch (6,019 run, none failed); the end-to-end and page tests of every path changed — the Build and Spectrum tabs, the charge and spin card, the renderer's own, and four Transport tab tests on a real junction (65 passed). |
| **2.69.47** | 2026-11-12 | **Which control edits a setting, decided once** — a last-number move ([`conventions.md`](?doc=process/conventions.md) § 2: fixes) — *(the overlap review's X-TMPL, its fifth finding)*. Which control edits a setting — a switch, a choice, a number, a triple — is the server's, tagged on every form field ([`web/form-schema.md`](?doc=web/form-schema.md) § 4, `_shared.control_for`). Task setup decided it again in the page from the setting's type, for its stage cells, run card rows and Measure adder alike (`legalValues`: a `bool` or an `enum` a dropdown, anything else a text box): two rules, agreeing on every setting either surface offers — measured over the catalogue, the one setting they part on, `tbt_spin`, an integer with a fixed choice, is fixed by its rung and offered on neither — but a setting with choices of another type would have been a dropdown on the form and a text box in its cell. **Now** both of Task setup's routes carry the server's control, as `control`, in place of the type, and `legalValues` reads it as Task setup draws it: a switch, whether or not it may stand unanswered, its two values; a choice, the members this kind offers; anything else a text box ([`web/task-setup.md`](?doc=web/task-setup.md) § 5.2). **What a person sees**: nothing. **Removed**: the routes' `type` field — its one reader, `legalValues`, reads `control`; `_shared._control_for`, now `control_for`, which the form's fields and Task setup's two routes ask. **Tests**: the test of the routes' value shape (`test_both_pickers_payloads_carry_the_value_shape`) asks the control — a switch a `checkbox`, one that may stand unanswered a three-way select, a fixed set of choices a `select` — seen failing on the old routes; the cell types' end-to-end test of a switch column gains a row, a switch that may stand unanswered (`scf_must_converge`), seen failing with the page's branch for it taken away; with every dropdown taken away the switch rows and a stored value not offered fail. Run through the project's runner: the fast batch on the final code (6,019 run, none failed); the end-to-end tests of Task setup's cells and its prep (35 passed before the review's row; with it, the cell types' file and the routes' test, 9 passed). |
| **2.69.46** | 2026-11-12 | **A kind's marker asked of the catalogue by the kind** — a last-number move ([`conventions.md`](?doc=process/conventions.md) § 2: fixes) — *(the overlap review's X-TMPL, its fourth finding)*. The four item keys `select` filters by kind name calculation kinds ([`engines/template.md`](?doc=engines/template.md) § 6.3, § 6.4): `calculations`, which kinds carry it — absent, every kind; `citation`, `role` and `shared`, which kinds a cited run answers it for, whose rungs fix it, whose stages it binds — absent, none. `select`, the one read API (§ 8.0), asked the last three yes or no only — whether some kind's — and read a kind given to them as a yes, so `select(role="optimization")` answered with the transport rungs' own items too; and the rule of `calculations` was written out again wherever one item was asked. So nine readers filtered the catalogue by a marker's kinds by hand — what a kind's rungs fix and answer, what binds its stages, what a transport's cited run answers, the shared settings Task setup lists, the form's role and shared fields — and eight wrote the `calculations` rule out again: whether a rung reads an item, why it does not, where a value may live, the catalogue's own load check and an item's own check of its stages, the form, `resolve` and the deck's walk. **Now** `select`'s `citation`, `role` and `shared` take a kind — or several — as well as yes or no, and one answer, `Item.carried_by`, says whether a kind carries an item, which `select(calculation=)` and every reader of a single item ask; `select` refuses a calculation given as several kinds, which answered with only the items every kind carries. **Measured** unchanged, before and after: every answer of the template's readers moved — whether a rung reads an item, why not, where a value may live, what a kind's rungs fix and answer, what binds its stages: 7,379, both engines, every kind, rung, item and home — the Build form's schema on every engine, kind and part, and fourteen PySCF decks; the cited run's two lists, Task setup's shared list, `resolve`'s and the load check's, read exactly equivalent. **What a person sees**: nothing. **Tests**: the test of `select`'s citation filter, on items written by hand, becomes rows over the real catalogue, both engines (`test_select_asks_a_kinds_marker_of_some_kind_or_of_one`) — each marker asked yes, no, or of a kind — and of several kinds at once — seen failing on the old `select` where a kind is not a yes: on SIESTA `role` of an optimization and `shared` of each kind, on PySCF `shared` of a transport; and a new test that a calculation given as several kinds is refused (`test_select_refuses_several_kinds_asked_as_one_calculation`), seen failing without the refusal. **Removed**: the hand-written citation test's two rows on three items written by hand, now rows over the catalogue. Run through the project's runner: the fast batch on the final code (6,019 run, none failed); no end-to-end run, every answer measured unchanged. |
| **2.69.45** | 2026-11-12 | **A PySCF deck's messages held in its words; the names it reports under spelled once** — a last-number move ([`conventions.md`](?doc=process/conventions.md) § 2: fixes) — *(what 2.69.44 left, by the user's ruling of 2026-11-12: "ignore the report-only text")*. A PySCF deck is the same calculation as another when its statements, what it only reports aside, are ([`execution/job-request.md`](?doc=execution/job-request.md) § 6). Nine messages a deck holds sat inside statements that also record or decide — the comment line each saved geometry carries (three, and the save helper's unused default), and a vibration's: the warning an unconverged relaxation records, the one an unrelaxed structure records with the way back in it, the install hint the infrared route is handed, the two reasons its thermochemistry is vibrational only, and the note on its full thermochemistry — so a release wording any of them otherwise refused every launched PySCF stage holding it prepared again — a saved geometry's line every PySCF stage, a vibration's messages every vibration. No rule over a deck's text can tell a message from a setting there: the optimization deck's atoms are a string of words. **Now** the deck holds its messages in its words, `_MB_WORDS` (`pyscf.script_input.WORDS_NAME`, one of the names it only reports under): each put there where it is composed and read from there where it is used, the statement that uses it unchanged by a rewording; the save helper takes the line every call hands it. Measured on rendered decks of both kinds: the worded text left compared is the optimization deck's atoms and the phase name the result records — what the deck computes and a value the result's readers read. **And every name a deck only reports under is spelled once** — its parameters record, runtime facts, convergence targets, settings copy, Methods text, bibliography, result-record version and words — in `pyscf.script_input`, which the deck's writers write with: measured over seven configurations of both kinds — twelve decks and two refusals — byte for byte the same before and after. **What changes in the files molbuilder writes**: a PySCF deck states its words near its head and reads each message from them; nothing it computes changes. **What a person sees**: a launched PySCF stage prepared again is not refused over a message a later release words otherwise. **A stage launched before this release is refused once**, prepared again for its job request: its deck held the messages in the statements that use them (measured on decks rebuilt as 2.69.44 wrote them — every one refused but an optimization that saves neither geometry). | **2.69.29** | 2026-11-11 | **Each browser tab its own saved work** — a last-number move ([`conventions.md`](?doc=process/conventions.md) § 2: fixes) — *(the user, 2026-11-11: "same module, one set per tab")*. This is the loss the plan had dropped on 2026-11-11 as seen only in the code: it was real. **What a person sees change**: after the upgrade nothing saved before is found once — each page opens as on a first visit. **Closing a tab now ends its set**: unsaved work comes back on a reload or within the tab, never in a new tab. A closed tab's files stay in the folder, unread — up to thirty undo points and its drafts each — and the folder now grows with every tab used; the server warns once it passes 300 files, as before. | **2.47.7** | 2026-10-28 | **A SIESTA vibration's optical calculation stops at 3 Ry, a cutoff said as one** — a last-number move ([`conventions.md`](?doc=process/conventions.md) § 2). *(the user: "it is an extremely high energy … go ahead with 3 Ry and fix the help text, clarify that this is a 'cut off' frequency for calculation")* `Optical.Energy.Maximum` defaulted to SIESTA's own 10 Ry (136 eV), taken unmeasured. **What it is**: the top of the photon-energy axis the optical calculation runs on — not a field and not an energy given to the molecule: a pair of states whose gap lies above it gets no dipole element computed (`Src/transition_rate.F:250`, `Src/optical.F:566`), and the spectrum is written up to it. **Measured on our runs** through molbuilder's own reader and Kramers–Kronig integral, cut against the full 10 Ry: the Raman derivative ∂α/∂Q moved 0.34 % or less at 3 Ry (CO₂'s 1250 cm⁻¹ stretch; a toy junction's 2548 cm⁻¹ mode, 0.00 %), α itself 1.1 % on CO₂; the spectra end where the basis's empty states end — 34 to 126 eV — and a DZP basis describes optical properties reasonably below about 10–20 eV (SIESTA's own optical tutorial), so the pairs far up are the confined orbitals' artefacts. **The cost**: in CO₂ half the pairs SIESTA computed at 10 Ry lie between 41 and 136 eV, the optical step was 58 % of the call, and each spectrum file is 30 % of the size. **The error of ours fixed with it**: the help said the range "saves only file size, since the cost is in the states, not the grid" — the cutoff prunes the pairs computed. **Now**: 3 Ry in the catalogue and the config class, the item named *Optical spectrum cutoff*, its help saying what the optical calculation is — every pair of an occupied and an empty state, its coupling to light along the chosen direction, placed at its gap: the absorption spectrum, from which the polarizability at the laser is one integral, each pair weighing about one over its gap squared — that this setting is the spectrum's top, and that it deviates from SIESTA's 10 Ry and why (`engines/template.md` § 5.2). A calculation already described keeps the value its template holds. The method's § 3.1 (*The cutoff*) holds the measurement; its checks no longer take the header's f-sum for a check of the range — it weighs each pair by its energy, so it reads lower under a cutoff by design ([`science/vibrational-optical-response.md`](?doc=science/vibrational-optical-response.md)); [`engines/vibration.md`](?doc=engines/vibration.md) § 5.11 states the 3 Ry and the sizes it gives. Tests: the prep protocol's row on the run's count and sizes, its spectrum 15,001 photon energies at the new default. Checked: the catalogue, template, vibration deck, form and prep tests. |

| **2.40.0** | 2026-10-25 | **A reuse answers a narrower question from the finished results; an extension has its own layout, its plan live before Describe** — a middle-number move ([`conventions.md`](?doc=process/conventions.md) § 2: new functionality). *(the user, 2026-10-25: "when you decide to extend a calculation, a lot of the parameters should no longer be changed, only a handful of things can be further changed, this is not clear in the UI design"; "build the layout, live plan before describe, when citing a multi frame calculation, first frame result is used ... and same checking for validate if this reuse is correct should still be in place"; "One rule: reuse completes")* **A narrower question** ([`engines/transport.md`](?doc=engines/transport.md) § 2a.16) — fewer voltages, or one geometry where the finished calculation ran a frame set (its first frame, the vibration's equilibrium geometry) — is answered from the finished results: a stage is copied in at prep when every result asked of it finished in the finished calculation's newest run, which has ended, computed the same way from the same inputs, and nothing of it runs; a calculation leaving self-consistent voltages for the low-bias approximation copies its 0 V results the same way. Where both calculations keep the results in the same folders, every run is copied whole and a voltage no longer asked stays in the copy, unread; otherwise the asked results are copied into this calculation's own folder layout as `run-0`, each with what it was given and what it continued from, every folder those records name moved to where this calculation keeps it — so a launch again takes the same inputs and a later calculation reusing this one takes its results over — a run of points stamped a container, its `tsgf/` folder made empty. The manifest names what a copy holds that is not asked: copied and never read, or left behind. **A result that did not finish is warned of** — each result of the finished calculation's newest run of a stage that did not finish, asked or not, named under its group with how it ended: it can mean a problem in the physics or in the parameter choices; the finished results are reused all the same *(the user, 2026-10-25: "A, keep it as built, but with warning of unfinished points because this could indicate problems in physics or parameter choices")*. An extension's voltages are the person's to add or remove. **An extension's own layout** on the Transport tab (§ 2a.16, *On the page*; [`web/tabs.md`](?doc=web/tabs.md) § 4), its cards headed as five parts: 1. *EXTENDING <name>* — the voltages, frames and energy window it describes — and the way out on its own row; 2. the junction, a choice of its own as it ran or a frame set of it; 3. what you can change — the voltages, the treatment, the device's and the transmission's tabs alone — with a value typed for it before, restored, said above the tabs with *Use <name>'s own values*; 4. *Fixed by <name>*, folded, view only; 5. the plan, then Describe. The shared panel, the charge-and-spin card, the seed's and the leads' tabs and the prose about citing are not shown; an unrelated citation leaves the extension and starts the voltages over. **The plan, live**: the describe door's `plan: true` writes the description into a hidden folder under the projects root, removed once answered, and answers what is copied and what is computed, asked again a moment after any change; an answer for what the page no longer holds is dropped. **Fixed before release, from two review rounds, a parameter audit and the transport e2e**: a copy into another folder layout lost its records of its inputs — the e2e's launch again of a copied device stopped in TranSIESTA for the leads' files; it was stamped a run where it held points, so the Results tab read it as queued; *Use <name>'s own values* wrote every blank field of the device's and the transmission's tabs as an explicit false, and the restored notice listed them as null; the way out turned a value nobody chose into one cited from the junction (the lock's `blank`); a newer change did not cancel a plan answer still on its way; the plan door answered a refused hidden folder with an HTML page. **CSV** is its variables as long tables, one per set of axes in each group (`frame_bias_v_energy_ev.csv` …), each axis a column under its own name, each variable a column named with its units, a value not stated an empty cell, with a `README.txt` in the variables' own words (`ncdata.as_tables`, any data file molbuilder writes, groups included, a group's tables keeping its parents' axes). **Two ways, one composition**: the transport report's **Export** (*CSV* · *NetCDF*, with *How to read these in Python*), a download through `GET /api/transport/export`; and `molbuilder jobset export --bundle <calc> --format csv|netcdf --to <folder>`, its folder required and never inside the calculation. **`<label>.transport.nc` is `molbuilder/transport-data@4`**: beside @3's, each spin's DOS, eigenchannels and printed current (`current_by_spin_a`, replacing `current_a_printed`), each spin's I–V current, each point's transmission k-points, how they were chosen and its loop time, what a point is a copy of and the energies it shares, each point's DOS-per-orbital reading, why a point is not done and how far it got, why a done point has no DOS, why the frames' average is none at each voltage (`average_why`, `average_conductance_why`), each run's state in the run door's sentence, every NEGF row's charges, each run's phases, a rung's copy, and as attributes the treatment's note, what each current means, the provenance chain and the calculation's own records (`description`, `template`, `job_request`). **The atom index map** (`model/overview.md` § 2.2b): the words *user index* (the user's structure's list) and *engine index* (the list a run's deck gives the engine); checked against the junction's record before the DOS by region or a selection's PDOS is read from it), the vibration result (**schema 10**) and **`spectra-data@2`**, a seeded trajectory log's `# atom_index_map:` line, `runtime-info`'s and `watch parse`'s answers, the structure sidecar (**v15**, its envelope's `atom_index_map`), an engine record's `.xyz` comment (read back, never the title); an engine record whose sidecar states no order, of a kind that may sort, is refused to an editor or a builder and opened marked for a viewer — the transport report draws such a junction as TranSIESTA lists it, its record's line saying so. An engine's own numbers are named as the engine's — the decks' comments, the force-constant facts' `numbering`, TranSIESTA's echoed electrode ranges as `runtime_info.transiesta.atom_numbers`; a PySCF vibration's Methods paragraph names its held atoms as every message does (`atom_words`). the Transport tab's reuse answer gave masses in the engine order beside a structure in the user order; `positions_ang`'s words said "as composed" of values in the user order; a page comment called the engine order the original; | **2.26.0** | 2026-10-20 | **Several groups as one job: a sequence, each group launched in turn when the one before has finished** — a middle-number move ([`conventions.md`](?doc=process/conventions.md) § 2: new functionality, and new files molbuilder writes). *(the user, 2026-10-20: "how about we allow submission of multiple groups in sequence for one job? … so the lower level logic stays the same, only at higher level we need to make sure the allocated hardware has the common factors/facility requested"; "so we can do jobset launch --groups 1,2,3"; "yes, write the contract, make sure parser, and jobset design are both correctly updated with framework and api level design/implmentation, result should understand this new structure")* **What it is** ([`job-system.md`](?doc=execution/job-system.md) § 6.0, *A sequence of groups*): `jobset launch task --group 1 --group 2 --group 3` sends ONE job whose walk launches each group named, in the description's order, as `launch task --group N --mode direct` would on the job's node -- each planned when its turn comes, so a transport device takes the seed and leads the job has just finished, under every gate of a first launch. Nothing below the group changed but what the job is: a group's walk, a run of points' walk, each run script, the ledger, the save before a group's first launch -- each run records the job it rode, and inside a queue job is told the job's wall from the job's start. A group that is refused or does not finish ends the job. One group at a time stays the default, and naming several is recorded as the decision to go on without looking between them. **Prep writes its header**: `prep task --group … --one-job` prepares the groups not launched and writes `launch/<label>_sequence-<every member's token>.sbatch` -- one queue; the largest ranks, cores per rank, GPUs and memory any group asks for; the walls summed -- its fit said, and each group asking less named (*runs with its own card inside it*): a transmission with GPU use off runs on its cores inside a GPU allocation. Two queues, or GPU groups asking two counts or two bindings, are refused (R-P22); a group a sequence still going holds is not prepared again under it (R-P23). **The launch** refuses before anything goes: a group not prepared, a group a sequence still going holds, no header prep wrote for exactly these groups, a header no longer what prep would write, a one-stage flag (R-L45). It shows the first group's plan in full and each later group's turn, then writes the walk `launch/<seq>.run.sh` and the sequence's launch record `launch/<seq>.launch.json` (`molbuilder/sequence-launch@1`: its groups, mode, line, placement, scheduler; when it went and the job's id, stamped by the sender) and sends the header -- or runs the walk here. Each step of the walk is `<molbuilder's python> -m molbuilder jobset launch task --group N --bundle <calculation> --mode direct --foreground --yes --in-sequence <seq> --in-job <the job's address>`, run from the folder molbuilder was imported from, which the job's node must see (R-L46: those two flags are the walk's). **Every run inside the job records the job it rode** (`run.json`'s new `sequence`, and the job's mode, line, id, placement and scheduler from the sequence's record), so every reader of a run -- the run door, the scheduler's answer written once, a run past its wall -- reads it as a run of that job, and a job cancelled reads its runs failed. The job's address (`<state dir>/jobs/`) holds its `sequence`, each group's launch adding its runs to it. **Inside the job**, a group's launch reads as a reader inside its own job (`joblist.inside`): a run of points its job sent is read by its points, never *running* or *queued* by asking that job, the gather's gates included; a group the description no longer gives as the sequence was sent with it (renumbered since) is refused, not launched as another (R-L46). **A group the job holds** (`ready.held_by_sequence`) is one answer for status, prep, launch, the bare launch's offer and the Results tab: held while the job is going and has launched it by neither its address nor the group's own runs' records; a job this machine cannot ask about holds nothing. **What the person sees**: `status` and the Results tab's ladder say *goes in job N -- after group K* for a group the job holds and has not reached; `molbuilder jobs status` names the job a sequence and lists the groups not launched yet; the Results tab's Run panel says *Sent by the sequence*; the run record carries `computation.launch.sequence`; the file catalogue a `<group>.launch.json` row. The one walk script's step can now be a command (`WalkCommand`), every other walk unchanged. **Checked by** an e2e with the real SIESTA (a two-stage H2 ladder sent as one sequence here and waited on: each stage finished, the second continuing from the first's run of the same job, each run naming the sequence; a second sent in the background: *goes in job N* in `status`, its group launched alone refused, `jobs status` listing it, `jobs stop` ending it) and API tests on a machine with queues (the header's largest ranks and summed walls, the walk and record written and the header sent, the refusals) -- each broken on purpose and seen to fail. |

A lead deck's `.validation.txt` says its seam. `<label>.transport.json` lists the points a stopped walk never reached as failed; `<label>.transport.nc`'s `iv_notes` is a list, each note placed by frame and voltage -- `summarize task` writes both again. *Carried from 2.25.1:* on a junction labelled the other way round, a device run made by 2.25.0 or earlier is refused at the transmission's gather (`TS.Elecs` is listed by label) -- launch the device again. **The contract** ([`engines/transport.md`](?doc=engines/transport.md)): what a frame set carries beyond its rows (`info.vibration` and the `mass_amu` channel, nothing else), the E_F reference asserted, the record's caveat as shipped, the kind gate's rows in § 6, the device facts after a warm `--gather`, a lead's period and seam, the principal layer TranSIESTA's own (I11) and a stop's meaning (§ 6.3), the run-of-points rule and its askers ([`execution/job-system.md`](?doc=execution/job-system.md)), a `die`'s reason kept ([`model/parse.md`](?doc=model/parse.md) § 2b), a period-one stack's seam ([`science/junction-cell.md`](?doc=science/junction-cell.md)), and a unitless row's unit ([`model/structure.md`](?doc=model/structure.md) § 2.2f). |
| **2.25.1** | 2026-10-20 | **The transport review's errors: a signed low-bias current, a frame set's record that reads, one rule for a run of points, one window rule, the person's deck zone kept, the leads listed by label** — a last-number move ([`conventions.md`](?doc=process/conventions.md) § 2: fixes). *(the user's yes, 2026-10-19: "a full-text, full-code review of transport.md and the frame-set contract against the code"; "Warn, worded truly"; "push when all green")* Three fresh reviewers read the transport contract whole against the code and SIESTA 5.4.2's source. **The errors, each by its mechanism.** Under the low-bias approximation the record took the voltage's magnitude for both leads' potentials (`record.linear_response_iv`), so I(−V) = +I(\|V\|): the current is signed now, I(−V) = −I(V), as TBtrans's own is. A frame set under the low-bias approximation crashed its record at the second frame -- a row offset reused the calculation folder's name. The transmission deck's gate and the description's preflight judged the window's edges while the record judged the energies TBtrans samples -- mid-rule points half a spacing inside each edge -- so a voltage near the edge passed prep and was then left out of the I–V: one rule now, on the sampled energies (`record.sampled_window`). The gather read a run of points by its own rule, in which a point a stopped chain never reached read *queued*, so a device whose walk stopped was refused as *still queued or running*: the status and the gather ask one rule now (`continuation.points_state` -- running while a point runs or between two points while the job that holds the run is going, queued while nothing it walks has started, else finished or failed naming the first point not done), which also ends the status's *failed* between two points. The gather's *deck now* left out the person's USER-CUSTOM zone, which prep keeps, so a keyword typed in a seed's or a lead's zone refused every later launch, and the way back brought the same zone again: the zone is merged in, as prep writes it. A junction labelled the other way round listed its leads by height, so TBtrans wrote only `R-L` and the record, reading `L-R`, found nothing: `TS.Elecs` lists them by label, each lead placed by its own `elec-pos` -- the usual junction's deck text unchanged. A frame set's vocabulary row in another unit was refused on the page only and written to the record and the data file under the vocabulary's unit: one check (`frame_average.check_units`) at all three. The catalogue stated the pole energy's engine default as 1.5 eV, but on the continued-fraction branch the device's decks take it is π·60·kT·0.7 (`m_ts_chem_pot.F90:313-319`). | **2.13.2** | 2026-10-18 | **A run is its directory, on the Modify tab too; three smaller corrections** — a last-number move ([`conventions.md`](?doc=process/conventions.md) § 2). *Lattice from a run* read a run's `.XV` by file name; it picks the relaxation run's folder, as the junction citation does, and reads the run through the run door — its last geometry step in the box it had ([`web-api.md`](?doc=web/web-api.md), `/api/modify/lattice-from-run`). A TBtrans loop is said *stopped* only in a final state, no longer for a run whose record does not read (`job.FINAL_STATES`). Task setup reads the hand-over file through one reader with its schema checked. |
| **2.13.1** | 2026-10-18 | **Two whole reads of contract against code — the transport calculation, and the result parser — and what they found, fixed** — a last-number move ([`conventions.md`](?doc=process/conventions.md) § 2). **A run cut inside an SCF block no longer reads "SCF converged"**: the reader kept the block before's `True` through a new block of the same phase; a new block's verdict is unanswered until it says (P-S2, [`model/parse.md`](?doc=model/parse.md)) — both runs synced from Sol, cancelled mid-iteration, read `—` now where they read *SCF yes*. **A polarized transmission point is done when it holds its two channel files**: the done-door asked for `.TBT.nc` alone, which TBtrans does not write on a polarized junction, so a polarized sweep read *0 of N done* and a warm relaunch walked every point again ([`engines/transport.md`](?doc=engines/transport.md) § 2a.11). The seed hands nothing on (its products were listing the device's `.TSDE`). The transport record states a gathered run's rung by the status door's one rule (`runstatus.points_verdict`). A launch record or a deck record that does not read answers `unreadable` on the Results tab's folder and contract doors, where it was a crash. A device with no point level is launched again as any stage, said where the contract contradicted itself (§ 2a.11, § 2a.15). The call table of `run_status` lists `run` and `launched_in`; a plain `.xyz` is said to open through the PySCF trajectory reader, as it does. |
| **2.13.0** | 2026-10-18 | **The version on every call and every page; the frames' post-process out of transport's execution** — a middle-number move ([`conventions.md`](?doc=process/conventions.md) § 2). *(the user, 2026-10-18: "let molbuilder show its version number whenever called from cli"; "and show the version number on the web ui too")* — every `molbuilder` command opens with `molbuilder <version>` on stderr, so what a command prints on stdout is untouched, and every page's header reads *molbuilder 2.13.0*. *(the user: "there is a post-process that should be done rather than put them together with the check and balances of transport itself")* — transport keeps its execution and its checks (the frames, a point each per voltage, every point and its rows recorded, the electrodes unmoved); **the frames' post-process** — their weighted average, its summary lines, the data file's frame part, what a page is answered of the frames, and a mode's vocabulary and what a harmonic mode's average assumes — is one module of its own, `frame_average.py`, which the record, the data file and the describe door call; `frameset.py` reads the rows as written and the weights, nothing else; `transport/average.py` is gone. Outputs are as they were, with three corrections found by the review of the multi-frame design: what a mode's average assumes is said only where the frames state a mode; frames stating rows but no weight are warned of; a structure row named like a frame row, a `mass_amu` channel that is no value channel, a true/false row, and a value a frame does not state no longer stop the describe door, the record or the data file ([`engines/transport.md`](?doc=engines/transport.md) § 2a.12, § 3.8.1; [`model/structure.md`](?doc=model/structure.md) § 2.2f). |
