The run door read SLURM's end-of-job line only where the launch record named its file, so a run launched before 2.12.0 — Sol's job 66027146, *CANCELLED AT 2026-10-18T13:58:37 DUE TO TIME LIMIT* — still read *running*. It reads the line where the job's header put it, the run's own folder under the file catalogue's name (`slurm.<id>.err`), whatever the record says ([`running-a-job.md`](?doc=execution/running-a-job.md) § 4.2). |
| **2.12.1** | 2026-10-18 | **A multi-frame structure is run frame by frame; its rows are its maker's** — a last-number move ([`conventions.md`](?doc=process/conventions.md) § 2) *(the user, 2026-10-18: "the frame can of course carry meta data, and why you treat it as transport's job to determine what should or should not be there?"; those rows "has nothing to do with fucking get calculation done")*. The transport citation no longer checks a frame set's rows against the coordinates nor refuses a set that states only some of them: what a calculation over the frames needs — the frames, one set of labels, box and `info.calculation`, the electrodes unmoved — is checked as before. **The one row it reads is each frame's `weight`**: where every frame states one the record averages at those weights; weights that do not sum to 1 are averaged as written, with a warning; frames that do not each state one have no average, said. **Everything else is an addition**: the record's `average` and the data file keep every key and variable they had — a mode's rows by their names, `mass_amu`, `info.vibration`, the page's words — and add every row as written (the `average`'s `rows`, the data file's `frame_rows` and `structure_rows`) and what is said of the weights (`warnings`, `frame_warnings`); a row the vocabulary does not know is shown by its name and unit. One frame read out of a set keeps its rows. Records and data files written before read as they are ([`model/structure.md`](?doc=model/structure.md) § 2.2f; [`engines/transport.md`](?doc=engines/transport.md) § 2a.9, § 2a.12, § 3.8.1). |
| **2.12.0** | 2026-10-18 | **How the scheduler ended a job is read where the run is** — a middle-number move ([`conventions.md`](?doc=process/conventions.md) § 2) *(the user, 2026-10-18: a run SLURM cancelled at its time limit read *still running*; "add a 'plugin layer' when submission mode is there and it can correctly decode the scheduler's message")*. **A job the scheduler stopped reads `failed`, in the scheduler's words**: SLURM's own line in the job's error file — `*** JOB 65999920 ON scc006 CANCELLED AT 2026-10-18T01:19:15 DUE TO TIME LIMIT ***` — read by its kind's reader and asked by the run door before the monitor's closing record, so it does not depend on a signal reaching anything: *stopped by the scheduler: CANCELLED AT 2026-10-18T01:19:15 DUE TO TIME LIMIT (slurm.65999920.err)*. `run.json` records the scheduler a job was sent to and where it writes the job's error file, from the run's own folder (`scheduler`), so the answer is the same on the cluster and wherever the run is copied; a record from before says nothing of it. A run of points' point asks it from its run's folder, where the one record lies, so a sweep the scheduler stopped reads `failed` at every point not done — and the next rung's prep no longer waits on it as *still running*. **The monitor counts the job's wall down**: from the wall `run.json` records and the job's start, which the header now states once for everything the job runs (`MB_JOB_STARTED`) — a walk's later member and a warm retry count from the job's start — each `[STATUS]` line says *wall 4h: 21m left*, and once, when the time left falls below the longest step the run has taken, it writes a `[WALL]` line and sends `wall_near`. **A header prepared before 2.12.0 is not what prep writes now**: prepare again before its first launch ([`scheduler.md`](?doc=execution/scheduler.md) R16, § 6a; [`running-a-job.md`](?doc=execution/running-a-job.md) § 4.2; [`run-reports.md`](?doc=execution/run-reports.md) § 2.1b, § 2.4). |
| **2.11.2** | 2026-10-18 | **The suite's result no longer depends on how many workers run it** — a last-number move ([`conventions.md`](?doc=process/conventions.md) § 2). Two tests of `test_describe.py` set `$MOLBUILDER_PROJECTS` by hand and deleted it after, whatever was there; since 2.11.0 spread a file's tests over the workers, which tests followed them in a worker changed with the worker count, and the two tests of the projects root's resolution (`test_seeding_a_fresh_machine.py`) passed or failed by it. They set it through `monkeypatch` now, which puts back what was there, and the suite clears your shell's `$MOLBUILDER_PROJECTS` for the session, so no test reads your tree ([`testing.md`](?doc=process/testing.md) § 2a). And the test of a spread run checked the rule 2.11.0 retired — every file whole on one worker — on a file whose tests share nothing, so it passed or failed by where xdist put them; it checks the rule now: a file whose tests share a fixture runs whole (§ 6.1a). |
| **2.11.1** | 2026-10-18 | **A playing movie asks the server nothing for the frames it passes** — a last-number move ([`conventions.md`](?doc=process/conventions.md) § 2) *(the user, 2026-10-18: "why does playing animation need a request to the server?")*. Playback moves the frame through the one write, so what follows the frame still redraws at each — drawing is local; what asks the server about the frame shown now waits for the frame playback stops on: MolView's view context writes nothing while the movie plays and writes once when it stops, and the transport report's charts follow each frame from its record while each selection's PDOS is asked once, at the stop. The viewer's handle announces playback once per start and once per stop (`onPlaybackChange`), never on a restart at a new speed ([`molview.md`](?doc=web/molview.md) § 9.2, § 11.2b; [`results.md`](?doc=web/results.md) § 2.5). |
| **2.11.0** | 2026-10-18 | **The machine's record is shown, never a veto** — a middle-number move ([`conventions.md`](?doc=process/conventions.md) § 2) *(the user, 2026-10-17: "the information on each domain is displayed correctly to the user, do not refuse user's choice, user knows best and when denied, the output gives the reason")*. **Prep no longer refuses a job for what the target's record says of the queue it names**: it prepares it and prints its **fit** — the nodes that hold the whole request, machine by machine, of how many, the machine sizes that hold it, and each limit it is over with its numbers (`900 GB fits 3 of 212 nodes of htc (1%): 96 cores · 1007.4 GB; 128 cores · 1511.2 GB`) — for each job it sends: a run, a run of points' walk, each benchmark shelf, a group's one job. The scheduler decides: its refusal at submit in its own words, `--mode ask` before, and `molbuilder jobs status` says the state and the scheduler's reason for a job it holds (`PENDING (QOSMaxWallDurationPerJobLimit)`) or has ended. Still refused: a value stated nowhere, a queue the record does not list, a scheduler whose record lists no queues, the GPU count rules, a deck that disagrees with its header. **The fit is said and recorded nowhere**: `job-set.json` is written as 2.10.3 wrote it, so a stage prepared then launches as it is. **A benchmark** keeps a cell declared whole — its ranks, cores per rank and, on the GPU, its device count — whatever its fit, and leaves out a cell the machine proposed that no node of its queue holds; the Task setup card fits the cells on the queue picked on the page, or on every queue while none is picked ([`generator.md`](?doc=execution/generator.md) § 4.3a). **What a queue shows**: its cores and memory as the ranges across its machines; `jobset probe` now writes a queue's one memory figure as its largest machine's — it wrote the smallest, so `--mem 900G` on Sol's `htc` was refused though three of its nodes have 1007 GB and more — read only where a queue lists no machines; a node's GPUs are every card it carries, counted together. **A launch's `--time`** is shown in the request the launch question prints, in place of the header's wall, the header's said beside it. **Re-probe each cluster** after pulling (`molbuilder jobset probe --write --name sol`) so its record's memory figure is its largest machine's ([`scheduler.md`](?doc=execution/scheduler.md) R0–R10, § 4–§ 7; [`job-system.md`](?doc=execution/job-system.md) § 6.0, § 6.0a). |
| **2.10.3** | 2026-10-17 | **`serve start` refuses a pidfile directory it cannot make, before it detaches** — a last-number move ([`conventions.md`](?doc=process/conventions.md) § 2). On a Sol compute node reached through SLURM, `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` named the login node's `/run/user/<uid>`, which is not there and cannot be made: the terminal said *starting in the background* and the detached supervisor died on its pidfile with a raw traceback. Now the refusal names the variable and the way out (unset it, or set it to a directory you own); one maker of that directory for `serve start`, the supervisor and the notebook ([`configuration.md`](?doc=configuration.md) § 2.1d, [`ops/deployment.md`](?doc=ops/deployment.md) § 1.0a). **The notebook through molbuilder's one port**: since 2026-09-15 the JupyterNB tab hid a notebook it could show — its check of the notebook's own port was blocked by the page's own security policy, and the tab read the block as *cannot reach it* (every version since, measured in a browser). Now JupyterLab listens on `127.0.0.1` under `/jupyter/`, and molbuilder passes `/jupyter/…` through on its own port, the kernel's WebSocket joined; the token never leaves the server; with no sign-in, this machine's browser passes only by a loopback name; and a restart of the server stops its notebook ([`web/jupyter.md`](?doc=web/jupyter.md) § 2, § 3.4, § 6). **After updating, `molbuilder serve stop` then `serve start`**: a running supervisor holds the notebook's old start command. |
| **2.10.2** | 2026-10-17 | **The GPU path, on a real GPU** — a last-number move ([`conventions.md`](?doc=process/conventions.md) § 2). **A calculation-wide run-card value reaches only the rungs that read it**: a transport calculation run on the GPU no longer makes its transmission rung — `tbtrans` has no GPU path — a GPU job holding a device it never uses; and a template that says `use_gpu = true` no longer leaves the transmission a GPU run with no count, refused with no way out ([`stages.md`](?doc=engines/stages.md) § 6.8d, [`execution/gpu.md`](?doc=execution/gpu.md) § 1.1). **A GPU run script says MPS as it decided**: its banner and its dry run said MPS on for one rank on one GPU, where it starts none; MPS is now one decision — allowed, its binary on PATH, a GPU visible, more ranks than GPUs — and the run's log says why it is off; `--mps` allows it rather than forcing it ([`running-a-job.md`](?doc=execution/running-a-job.md) § 3.3). The comments over a GPU run's rank binding no longer claim the whole-job `numactl` wrap holds its ranks to the GPU's socket — measured, it does not; a rank away from its GPU is warned about, never refused, and the warning no longer blames a scheduler ([`execution/gpu.md`](?doc=execution/gpu.md) G10). |
| **2.10.1** | 2026-10-17 | **Step 1's last fixes: four places a file said what did not happen** — a last-number move ([`conventions.md`](?doc=process/conventions.md) § 2). **A SIESTA deck with more than one k-point on ELPA**, the default solver, now writes `Diag.ParallelOverK .false.` — SIESTA runs ELPA over the orbitals only and turned the split off itself, while every such deck, a transport lead's among them, said `.true.` — SIESTA's manual calls ELPA not compatible with it; set on with ELPA, it is written as set and prep warns ([`engines/siesta.md`](?doc=engines/siesta.md) § 6.1). **A pseudopotential is copied by the exact name SIESTA opens**, `<label>.psml`, the rule every check asks — a near match (`au.psml`, or `Cl.psml` for a missing `C.psml`) is no longer copied under that name. **A PySCF deck no longer promises `_geom.log`**, which geomeTRIC never writes here: its prefix is derived from the trajectory it does write, `_geom_optim.xyz`, now a declared run file a cold restart keeps as its run's history ([`engines/pyscf.md`](?doc=engines/pyscf.md) § 2). **A vibration compares the k-point grid** its deck will write with the one its structure was relaxed at, beside the basis and the mesh cutoff ([`engines/vibration.md`](?doc=engines/vibration.md) § 2.2). **A junction cited from a pair refuses an electrode atom its relaxation did not hold**, as the record says, whatever the label says now ([`engines/transport.md`](?doc=engines/transport.md) § 1.1). **Prep decides; launch sends what prep wrote** *(the user, 2026-10-17: "all the decision is already done at the prep … Launch means just run that script")*: the queue, ranks, cores, GPUs and memory are decided at prep from the target's record and written into the job's one header — a stage's, a group's, a run of points' (one job walking every point, their walls summed) and a benchmark's shelves', each admitted on its queue at prep; a launch admits nothing and picks no queue — `launch --domain`, `--mem` and `--gpu-domain` are gone, and a submitted job's wall is the one value a launch may restate (`--time`). Prep prints every value of each header beside where it came from, and a run's GPU sharing; launch shows every value of the header it sends. A benchmark measures on the one queue its prep names: it says where it was placed and the queue's cap on submitted jobs, its GPU cells on a queue with no GPUs are crossed out by that queue's own refusal, prepared with `--no-sbatch` its cells are the target's probed node's, and the Task setup card checks the same queue. A header a prep does not write — a run of points' per-point ones among them — is taken away, so no earlier header is ever sent. A run on a scheduler that names no queue is refused at prep with the target's queues listed against what the run asks, its GPUs among it ([`job-system.md`](?doc=execution/job-system.md) § 6.0, [`scheduler.md`](?doc=execution/scheduler.md) R1, R9). **A scheduler is a kind, in one file** *(the user, 2026-10-17: "unified layer, unified API, unified protocol")*: everything molbuilder says to a scheduler — the line, its ask, the job id, the header, the wall a launch restates, what it tells a running job — is `scheduler/kinds.toml`'s, and the machine's record names the kind and where its commands are (`environment@3`): `jobset probe` finds them on PATH, or `--set scheduler.submit=PATH` declares a site's own wrapper. Launch runs the command the calculation's copy of the record names; the header says what the scheduler tells the job once in molbuilder's names (`MB_NTASKS`, …), so the run script is one file on every machine. **Re-probe each machine** (`molbuilder jobset probe --write`, on Sol too): an older record is refused by name; a prepared calculation's copy is rewritten by `jobset migrate` ([`scheduler.md`](?doc=execution/scheduler.md) § 6a, [`configuration.md`](?doc=configuration.md) § 5 M-2). |
| **2.10.0** | 2026-10-14/16 *(RMP, plan § 5ab — R1 to R6, R4 dropped)* | **New: the order a structure's atoms are in is recorded, and you are always shown and given your own** — a middle-number move, new functionality ([`conventions.md`](?doc=process/conventions.md) § 2; [`model/overview.md`](?doc=model/overview.md) § 2.2a). A structure an engine needs in another order — a SIESTA vibration's held atoms first, TranSIESTA's electrode blocks — carries its **order record**, `atom_order`, the key that made it and both directions: in the `.molstruct.json` (**sidecar v14**, `null` in your order; v7–v13 read as your order) and in every deck written from it (its ATOM-METADATA block, which now carries every metadata field but the box, and is written whenever it has a label, a channel, a named value or an order record — a block of named values alone was dropped by the run door). **Every door that crosses back restores**: the Molbuilder tab's load and Add, `molbuilder modify` (its agreement saying the atom indices count in your order), the save, the download and the hand-over, `jobset init`, `xv2xyz --from-run` — each saying so (*… was in an engine's order (held atoms first); it is put back in your order*); no pair is written for a person in an engine's order (the codec refuses, but for the transport composer's junction). **A viewer holding a run in its engine's order says so** above its tabs; SIESTA's own `<label>.xyz` in a run opens through the run's view, with its labels and order; a bare `xv2xyz` of a reordered run's `.XV` is refused, naming `--from-run`; `watch parse`, `watch tail` and `runtime-info` say when a file's run is in its engine's order. **A deck prepared before 2.10.0** for a kind that reorders states no order: `xv2xyz`, `jobset init`, `molbuilder modify` and the Molbuilder tab's open refuse it; the Results tab and the bare readers say so, and a save, download or hand-over from the Results tab writes it as the engine listed it; launch, refusing a stage prepared from another description, names the molbuilder that prepared it — prepare it again. **A SIESTA vibration's force-constant stage takes the relaxation's coordinates through the run's own order record**; prep and a transport citation refuse a run prepared before 2.10.0 whose order nothing states. **A calculation's structure edited after it was described** — a coordinate, a label, a held atom, the box — is refused at prep: the description records the structure's fingerprint (describe it again to build on the edit). **A transport calculation citing a run that relaxed no geometry** — a vibration's force-constant stage, a transport rung — is refused by name. Every message that names atoms of a deck's sorted copy — a held set's warning, an atom outside the box, a lead that moved — names them in your order. The relaxation record drops `held_atom_idxs` (its held atoms are named by their lines). **`atom-permutation.json` is no longer written**: a SIESTA vibration's finish reads its order record from its own deck, and a transport calculation's composition record from its junction's sidecar. **A transport calculation's data file states its atoms in your order** (`molbuilder/transport-data@2`); its record names no permutation file; a vibration's force-constant range is named as SIESTA's numbering, `fc_range_engine_1based`, in its result (`schema_version` 8; a v7 result still reads, its range under the new name) and in the displacement sweep's record (`molbuilder/fc-displacement-sweep@2`) — `summarize task` writes the records again. **A calculation carries across an upgrade of molbuilder** (UPG): a run is taken by the next stage when it ran the same engine input as the deck its stage renders now — comments and molbuilder's records aside, an explicit engine default (the catalogue's `engine_default`, with its source line) the same as none, the geometry within 10⁻⁶ Å — and the gather's refusal names the first difference, never a version; what a file holds is read from what it states, never from the molbuilder that wrote it. A transport junction whose sidecar states no order record is composed again in memory by the next prep and, the same junction atom for atom, given its order record — `prep task` with no group left to prepare records it too; a prep never replaces a junction a launched stage ran on. Until then its data file and a selection's PDOS are refused by name, naming `prep task`, and the report says so (`molbuilder/transport-result@7`). **Every route's path fence, asked of every route**: a `psml_lib` whose `..` climbs out of the projects tree is refused like an absolute one outside it, and Prep's `from` is fenced at the route. **A 3-D viewer put away takes its drag-select listener off the page**: it stayed on the document, one more for every viewer opened. **A 3-D viewer closed no longer stays in memory for the page's life**: its drawing library has no way to take a window down, so every file opened in a 3-D viewer left one behind, listening to the page; the viewers now keep the page's drawing windows and lend one, cleared, to each viewer opened — a page holds as many as were ever open at once ([`web/molview.md`](?doc=web/molview.md) § 9.9a). **The fcc metals' PBE lattice constants come from a paper anyone can open**: Zhang et al. 2018 (all-electron, nickel ferromagnetic) — Au 4.157, Ag 4.149, Cu 3.631, Ni 3.518, Pt 3.970, Pd 3.942 Å, each within 0.004 Å of the values before, which matched no source that could be read; a slab built on the PBE reference moves by at most that (`molbuilder/data/README.md`'s table now states both columns and their sources). **A vibration's animation closed no longer stays in memory**: every spectrum opened left the animation's drawing window behind, listening to the page, and past sixteen the browser took a graphics context from a view on screen; the animation now keeps its drawing windows and lends one, cleared, to each it opens ([`web/vibrationview.md`](?doc=web/vibrationview.md) § 9.3a). **The trajectory viewer never draws another file's frames**: when the server answered a live run's poll for another file, the viewer kept the answer out of its record but could still append that file's frames to the movie on screen; an answer it refuses now draws nothing. Its status line is its own panel's, so two viewers on a page cannot write into each other's. **Reload on the Results tab opens the file on screen again, once**: a trajectory or a spectrum was read twice — reloaded in place, then put away and mounted afresh — and the first read thrown away; now every viewer is mounted afresh, as it already was last. **The preview of a file whose highlighting cannot be loaded shows it as plain text**, never in the last file's highlighting — and so does a file large enough to be paged in, which kept the last file's highlighting. **Each group's next step**: `status` and `launch task` end with every group, where it stands and the line that moves it, and the Results tab shows it under a calculation's ladder, saying when it was read. **The transport report draws its junction in your order**, and a projected-DOS selection is named in your order, asked and answered (`/api/transport/pdos`). **A transmission says its loop as TBtrans states it** (the user: *"the parser should have multiple ways to determine the status of the run for tbtrans rather than relying on one estimation line"*): the one SIESTA-family reading pass reads every line TBtrans prints about its k-point × energy loop — its size, the spin passes it says it will make, each pass's data file, TBtrans's estimate while a pass runs (*Calculated N %, ETA in S s*, every `TBT.Progress` per cent — a new item, 5, tbtrans's own default, written in the deck), the time each pass *Completed in*, its currents — and the monitor's status line and messages, the run's record and Run panel (`computation.time.progress`, with when the estimate was made while one stands) and the transport report say it in one sentence ([`model/parse.md`](?doc=model/parse.md) § 5d.5): *setting up its loop: 113 k-points × 401 energies, 1 pass (unpolarized)*; *spin up (1 of 2): 40 % done, about 3h left (TBtrans's estimate), then spin down*; *completed: spin up (1 of 2) in 6h, spin down (2 of 2) in 6h*; *stopped during spin down (2 of 2) at 40 %; spin up (1 of 2) completed in 6h*. The monitor writes a `[STATUS]` line as TBtrans's account moves — its set-up read, a progress line, a pass begun or completed, its currents. A finished run no longer reads 98 % done with 0 s left, and a second spin pass is named, not read as the first beginning again at 0 %; a TranSIESTA device's own contours are never read as a transmission's. The report's transmission row names its running point, how many points are still to come after it and how long the last finished point's loop took — a point to come is timed by nothing else; a stopped point says where it stopped. A transmission run once, at one voltage, carries its point (`0 V`) on the ladder's row, as a swept one does. The monitor's report fields `done_pct` and `eta_s` are the pass underway's, and a card says a share as the engine printed it (`10.01 %`, its trailing zeros dropped). `<label>.transport.json` is `molbuilder/transport-result@8`: a finished point's `loop_elapsed_s`, a point not finished its `progress`, the transmission rung's `progress` — `summarize task` writes it again. `mb_monitor.pyz`, written beside every job, carries `tbtrans.py`, the loop's words. The TBtrans items' manual references name the sections they are in, and `TBT.Progress`'s help says what the surfaces show. A transmission k-point grid set above the Gamma point while the shared grid is the Gamma point alone is warned at prep — tbtrans stops on it in its set-up, the device's Hamiltonian carrying no supercell — and left to the person (the user: *"let tbtrans handle it. Prep can detect and warn but user decide what to do"*). `read_tbtrans_out`, a second walk of the same output, is gone: the currents are the reading pass's, each beside its pass's channel. **A length of time a run says of itself is said one way** — `12s`, `5m`, `6h`, `6d`, on the page and in the monitor's messages alike, the trajectory badge's *total* among them (*83m*, where it said *1h 23m*); a benchmark's measured times keep the second ([`web/ui-contract.md`](?doc=web/ui-contract.md) § 4). **A run here starts in the background** (the user: *"make it default but also explicitly changable in the question"*): `launch --mode direct` starts it and returns, saying where its output is and how to stop it (`molbuilder jobs stop <id>`, below); asked at a terminal, *launch in the background? [Y/n/foreground(F)]* — `f` runs it in the foreground, waited on, the launch ending with how it ended; `--foreground` makes that the plan, the question then offering the background; `--background` is gone, and `--foreground` on a queue — or a queue's flag on a run here, `--domain`, `--time`, `--mem`, `--gpu-domain` — is refused with the launch as typed for each mode. What it says: *about to start here, in the background:* or *about to run here, waited on:*, a dry run's *WOULD start in the background* or *WOULD run*, and for a run here either way the folder to read. The ledger keeps the question as asked and its answer. **`molbuilder jobs status` and `molbuilder jobs stop <id>`** (the user: *"can we keep the status of running jobs the same way as we keep status of running server"*): every launch leaves its address in molbuilder's state folder, `jobs/` — a run here's host, pid and start, a queue job's id, the calculation and each member's run — and `jobs status` reads each fresh, its process or the scheduler and each member's run as `jobset status` reads it, a job that has ended said once and then dropped; `jobs stop` shows what it stops, asks *stop it? [y/N]*, checks again that the run is the process launched, sends SIGTERM to a background run's process group (or `scancel`s a queue job, asked on its own cluster) and writes the question, its answer and the stop in the calculation's ledger. A run left in the background is printed with its line, `molbuilder jobs stop <id>`; a run waited on leaves no address once its launch has said how it ended ([`execution/job-system.md`](?doc=execution/job-system.md) § 6.0a). A walk's own errors — a start that could not be copied among them — are in its log under `launch/`, and the launch names a group's or a sweep's walk log ([`execution/job-system.md`](?doc=execution/job-system.md) § 6.0). **`copy_psml` is retired**: nothing read it — prep always copies a calculation's pseudopotentials beside its decks. Every SIESTA template written before 2.10.0 holds it, and prep refuses such a template by name until `molbuilder jobset migrate` takes it out, keeping the old file as `<name>.pre-2.10.0` and carrying the rest whole — every value and blank, each item's source as the file records it; migrate also renames an item a release renamed, in a stage's own values and `varies` too, takes out PySCF's retired `optimizer = "geometric"` and any blank retired item, says what it cannot carry (an item no longer of the kind, a person's comments inside the file), and refuses a retired value that no longer runs, one item at two values under its two names, and a name no release held. Every door that reads a name — the template reader, the description's check, a stage's overrides — says a retired or renamed one in one sentence, naming `jobset migrate` ([`engines/template.md`](?doc=engines/template.md) § 3). **The Watch tab, pointed at a folder whose result is no trajectory** — a transmission run's, its calculation's report; a spectrum's — says so, naming that file and the Results tab, which shows it. **A transmission run's folder opens its calculation's report at its point**: the Result file menu lists `task.json` first and opens it with the folder's frame and voltage selected, as the ladder row's *open* does, the header saying it is the folder's result, the meta line giving the report its age, and the file card saying what `task.json` is; a return to the tab keeps the point you picked since; it opened the progress log the wrapper writes at launch — one preview frame, never updated by TBtrans — and a running transmission showed a structure and no plot ([`web/results.md`](?doc=web/results.md) § 2). **A structure pair is written whole or not at all**: both halves are rendered before either is written, so a sidecar that cannot be — a NaN recorded in `info` — leaves the pair on disk as it was, where it used to leave the new coordinates beside the old sidecar ([`model/structure.md`](?doc=model/structure.md) § 2.4). **A PDB's title keeps to its columns**: a title longer than seventy characters continues on further numbered `TITLE` records, broken between words, where it used to run past column 80 — and reads back as it was written. **The Cell page says what the boundary does to the crystal**: committing a box answers the slab build's seam verdict on it — `collision`, `continues`, `vacuum`, a twin or an eclipse, and the box's own collision with a molecule in it — so setting `c` after building a slab is answered where it is set ([`science/junction-cell.md`](?doc=science/junction-cell.md) § 6.1). **`molbuilder validate` with no `--engine` checks the box too**: a cell with no volume or a mirrored one is an error whatever runs on it, as with an engine named. **A TranSIESTA device's forces are said not computed**: TranSIESTA computes none on molbuilder's device and says its forces are wrong, so the device's status line, run record and trajectory plot say *TranSIESTA does not compute forces in this run (it says they are wrong), and so not the stress either* where a force of 0.09 eV/Å was shown. **A run's status line names the held atoms' force after the tolerance when it is the larger** (*max force 2.272 eV/Ang (tol 0.01); 3.211 eV/Ang on the held atoms*), its report `max_force_held`; the run record keeps the last step's forces and its stress tensor (`verdict.max_force_free_ev_ang`, `.max_force_held_ev_ang`, `.stress_voigt_kbar`), the Run panel showing the stress in the (x, y) plane ([`model/parse.md`](?doc=model/parse.md) § 5d.6). *A pair saved before 2.10.0 from a reordered run's view is in the engine's order with nothing to say so, and cannot be told apart.* |

The Results tab's file card, for a file of a calculation whose `task.json` its reader refuses, says whose file it is cannot be said there and gives the reader's own words, the way out among them (`jobset migrate`) — it said *not written by molbuilder* for every file ([`web/results.md`](?doc=web/results.md) § 3b). A live run's movie follows a poll that rebuilds it — the trajectory viewer's install says it replaces what its read-only viewer shows (`enforce`, [`web/molview.md`](?doc=web/molview.md) § 9.4); every rebuild after the first load, and every Reload, was refused in silence, the frame counter growing over the old frames ([`web/trajectory.md`](?doc=web/trajectory.md) § 5). **SIESTA's history is each run's own**: `.MD.nc`, `.MD`, `.MDE` and `.ANI` are named for the run that wrote them — `<label>_<stage>-run<N>.MD.nc` and its three siblings, in either shape — the run script pointing SIESTA's name for each at the run's file while the engine runs and removing the pointer when it exits ([`execution/job-contracts.md`](?doc=execution/job-contracts.md) § 2.2, § 2.6); a continuing stage carries none (`siesta/warm-files.toml`), and a run's frames are sharpened from its own history — a flat stage launched again took the earlier run's rows ([`model/parse.md`](?doc=model/parse.md) § 5a). A pair saved before 2.9.6 opens unchecked.
